### अकारांत पुल्लिंग – बालक
|विभक्ति|एक.|द्वि.|बहु.|
|प्रथमा|बालकः|बालकौ|बालकाः|
|द्वितीया|बालकम्|बालकौ|बालकान्|
|तृतीया|बालकेन|बालकाभ्याम्|बालकैः|
|चतुर्थी|बालकाय|बालकाभ्याम्|बालकेभ्यः|
|पंचमी|बालकात्|बालकाभ्याम्|बालकेभ्यः|
|षष्ठी|बालकस्य|बालकयोः|बालकानाम्|
|सप्तमी|बालके|बालकयोः|बालकेषु|
### आकारांत स्त्रीलिंग – माला
|विभक्ति|एक.|द्वि.|बहु.|
|प्रथमा|माला|माले|मालाः|
|द्वितीया|मालाम्|माले|मालाः|
|तृतीया|मालया|मालाभ्याम्|मालाभिः|
|चतुर्थी|मालायै|मालाभ्याम्|मालाभ्यः|
|पंचमी|मालायाः|मालाभ्याम्|मालाभ्यः|
|षष्ठी|मालायाः|मालयोः|मालानाम्|
|सप्तमी|मालायाम्|मालयोः|मालासु|
### अकारांत नपुंसकलिंग – फल
- प्रथमा: फलम्, फले, फलानि; द्वितीया: फलम्, फले, फलानि (प्रथमा-द्वितीया समान); शेष बालक की तरह – फलेन, फलाय, फलात्, फलस्य, फले।
### ईकारांत स्त्रीलिंग – नदी
- प्रथमा: नदी, नद्यौ, नद्यः; द्वितीया: नदीम्, नद्यौ, नदीः; तृतीया: नद्या, नदीभ्याम्, नदीभिः।
- चतुर्थी: नद्यै; पंचमी-षष्ठी: नद्याः; षष्ठी बहु.: नदीनाम्; सप्तमी: नद्याम्, नद्योः, नदीषु।
### उकारांत पुल्लिंग – साधु/गुरु
- प्रथमा: साधुः, साधू, साधवः; द्वितीया: साधुम्, साधू, साधून्; तृतीया: साधुना।
- चतुर्थी: साधवे; पंचमी-षष्ठी: साधोः; षष्ठी द्वि.: साध्वोः, बहु.: साधूनाम्; सप्तमी: साधौ, साधुषु।
### ऋकारांत – पितृ व मातृ
- पितृ: पिता, पितरौ, पितरः; पितरम्, पितरौ, पितॄन्; पित्रा; पित्रे; पितुः; पितुः, पित्रोः, पितॄणाम्; पितरि, पितृषु।
- मातृ: माता, मातरौ, मातरः; द्वितीया बहु. मातॄः; मात्रा; मात्रे; मातुः; मातॄणाम्; मातरि।
! पितृ-मातृ में अंतर केवल द्वितीया बहुवचन: पितॄन् / मातॄः।
### सर्वनाम – तत्, एतत्, किम्
- तत् (पुं.): सः, तौ, ते; तम्, तौ, तान्; तेन; तस्मै; तस्मात्; तस्य, तयोः, तेषाम्; तस्मिन्, तेषु।
- तत् (स्त्री.): सा, ते, ताः; ताम्; तया; तस्यै; तस्याः; तासाम्; तस्याम्, तासु।
- तत् (नपुं.): तत्, ते, तानि (प्रथमा-द्वितीया)।
- एतत् (पुं.): एषः, एतौ, एते; एतम्; एतेन; एतस्मै; एतस्य; एतस्मिन्।
- किम्: पुं. कः, नपुं. किम्; कस्मै, कस्मात्, कस्य, कस्मिन्।
### अस्मद् व युष्मद्
|विभक्ति|अस्मद् (एक./द्वि./बहु.)|युष्मद् (एक./द्वि./बहु.)|
|प्रथमा|अहम्, आवाम्, वयम्|त्वम्, युवाम्, यूयम्|
|द्वितीया|माम्, आवाम्, अस्मान्|त्वाम्, युवाम्, युष्मान्|
|तृतीया|मया, आवाभ्याम्, अस्माभिः|त्वया, युवाभ्याम्, युष्माभिः|
|चतुर्थी|मह्यम्, आवाभ्याम्, अस्मभ्यम्|तुभ्यम्, युवाभ्याम्, युष्मभ्यम्|
|पंचमी|मत्, आवाभ्याम्, अस्मत्|त्वत्, युवाभ्याम्, युष्मत्|
|षष्ठी|मम, आवयोः, अस्माकम्|तव, युवयोः, युष्माकम्|
|सप्तमी|मयि, आवयोः, अस्मासु|त्वयि, युवयोः, युष्मासु|

## धातु रूप
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree धातु रूप – माइंड मैप
धातु रूप
  10 गण
    भ्वादि … चुरादि
  3 पद
    परस्मैपदी – पठति
    आत्मनेपदी – सेवते
    उभयपदी – करोति/कुरुते
  5 प्रमुख लकार
    लट्, लृट्, लङ्, लोट्, विधिलिङ्
  प्रमुख धातुएँ
    पठ्, भू, गम्, लिख्, कृ, सेव्
@end

### धातु, गण व पद
@tree धातु के पद
पद
  परस्मैपदी – पठति (पठ्, लिख्, गम्)
  आत्मनेपदी – सेवते (सेव्, मुद्, लभ्)
  उभयपदी – करोति/कुरुते (कृ, ब्रू, पच्)
@end

- धातु = क्रिया का मूल रूप (पठ्, भू, गम्, कृ)।
- 10 गण (पहली धातु के नाम पर): भ्वादि (भू, पठ्, गम्), अदादि (अद्), जुहोत्यादि (हु), दिवादि (दिव्), स्वादि (सु), रुधादि (रुध्), तुदादि (तुद्), तनादि (तन्, कृ), क्र्यादि (क्री, ग्रह्, ज्ञा), चुरादि (चुर्, कथ्)।
- परस्मैपदी: पठति (पठ्, लिख्, गम्); आत्मनेपदी: सेवते (सेव्, मुद्, लभ्); उभयपदी: करोति/कुरुते (कृ, ब्रू, पच्)।
- लौकिक संस्कृत में मुख्यतः लट्, लृट्, लङ्, लोट्, विधिलिङ्।
### परस्मैपदी प्रत्यय (5 लकार)
|लकार|प्रथम पुरुष|मध्यम पुरुष|उत्तम पुरुष|
|लट्|ति, तः, अन्ति|सि, थः, थ|मि, वः, मः|
|लृट्|स्यति, स्यतः, स्यन्ति|स्यसि, स्यथः, स्यथ|स्यामि, स्यावः, स्यामः|
|लङ्|त्, ताम्, अन्|स्, तम्, त|अम्, व, म|
|लोट्|तु, ताम्, अन्तु|–, तम्, त|आनि, आव, आम|
|विधिलिङ्|इत्, इताम्, इयुः|इः, इतम्, इत|इयम्, इव, इम|
- आत्मनेपदी लट्: ते, इते, अन्ते; से, इथे, ध्वे; ए, वहे, महे।
### प्रमुख धातु रूप (प्रथम पुरुष एकवचन)
|धातु|लट्|लृट्|लङ्|लोट्|विधिलिङ्|
|पठ् (पढ़ना)|पठति|पठिष्यति|अपठत्|पठतु|पठेत्|
|भू (होना)|भवति|भविष्यति|अभवत्|भवतु|भवेत्|
|गम् (जाना)|गच्छति|गमिष्यति|अगच्छत्|गच्छतु|गच्छेत्|
|लिख् (लिखना)|लिखति|लेखिष्यति|अलिखत्|लिखतु|लिखेत्|
|कृ (करना)|करोति|करिष्यति|अकरोत्|करोतु|कुर्यात्|
|सेव् (सेवा) आत्मने.|सेवते|सेविष्यते|असेवत|सेवताम्|सेवेत|
- कृ लट्: करोति, कुरुतः, कुर्वन्ति; करोषि, कुरुथः, कुरुथ; करोमि, कुर्वः, कुर्मः।
- कृ लोट् मध्यम: कुरु; उत्तम: करवाणि।

## उपसर्ग एवं अव्यय (संस्कृत)
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree उपसर्ग एवं अव्यय (संस्कृत) – माइंड मैप
उपसर्ग एवं अव्यय
  उपसर्ग (22)
    धातु से पहले
    अर्थ बदलते हैं
    हार → प्रहार, आहार…
  अव्यय
    कालवाचक
    स्थानवाचक
    रीतिवाचक
    अन्य
@end

### उपसर्ग (22)
@tree उपसर्ग से अर्थ परिवर्तन (√हृ)
हार
  प्र – प्रहार
  आ – आहार
  सम् – संहार
  वि – विहार
  परि – परिहार
@end

- धातु से पहले जुड़कर अर्थ बदलते हैं; स्वतंत्र प्रयोग नहीं।
- "उपसर्गेण धात्वर्थो बलादन्यत्र नीयते। प्रहाराहार-संहार-विहार-परिहारवत्॥" – 'हार' से प्रहार, आहार, संहार, विहार, परिहार।
- 22 उपसर्ग: प्र, परा, अप, सम्, अनु, अव, निस्, निर्, दुस्, दुर्, वि, आङ्, नि, अधि, अपि, अति, सु, उत्, अभि, प्रति, परि, उप।
- प्रयोग: प्रहार, पराजय, अपहरण, संहार, अनुकरोति, अवतरति, निर्गच्छति, विहार, आगच्छति, अधिराजते, अत्याचार, अभिगच्छति, प्रत्युपकार, परिवर्तन, उपगच्छति।
### अव्यय (संस्कृत)
@tree अव्यय के प्रकार
अव्यय
  कालवाचक
    अद्य, ह्यः, श्वः
    इदानीम्/अधुना
  स्थानवाचक
    अत्र, तत्र, कुत्र
    पुरतः, परितः
  रीतिवाचक
    कथम्, शनैः, सहसा
    साकम्/सह, अलम्
  अन्य
    अपि, न, वा, एव
    खलु, धिक्, तूष्णीम्
@end

- "सदृशं त्रिषु लिङ्गेषु सर्वासु च विभक्तिषु। वचनेषु च सर्वेषु यन्न व्येति तदव्ययम्॥"
- कालवाचक: अद्य (आज), ह्यः (बीता कल), श्वः (आने वाला कल), यदा-तदा, पुरा (पहले), प्रातः, सायम्, नक्तम् (रात), इदानीम्/अधुना/सांप्रतम् (अब), कदा, सदा, चिरम्।
- स्थानवाचक: अत्र (यहाँ), तत्र (वहाँ), यत्र, कुत्र/क्व (कहाँ), सर्वत्र, अन्यत्र, अन्तः, बहिः, इतः, ततः, पुरतः (सामने), परितः (चारों ओर)।
- रीतिवाचक: यथा-तथा, कथम् (कैसे), शनैः (धीरे), सहसा (अचानक), साकम्/सह (साथ), वृथा (व्यर्थ), अलम् (पर्याप्त/निषेध)।
- अन्य: अपि (भी), न (नहीं), वा (या), हि (क्योंकि), अहो (आश्चर्य), नूनम् (निश्चित), स्वयम्, पुनः, एव (ही), च (और), तु/किन्तु, खलु (निश्चय ही), भोः (हे), धिक्, तूष्णीम् (चुप), मुहुर्मुहुः (बार-बार), नमः।

## संधि (संस्कृत)
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree संधि (संस्कृत) – माइंड मैप
संधि
  परिचय
    परः सन्निकर्षः संहिता
    नित्य व वैकल्पिक
    आदेश, आगम, लोप
  स्वर (अच्) संधि
    दीर्घ, गुण, वृद्धि, यण्
    अयादि, पूर्वरूप, पररूप, प्रकृतिभाव
  व्यंजन (हल्) संधि
    श्चुत्व, ष्टुत्व, जश्त्व, चर्त्व
    अनुस्वार, परसवर्ण, लत्व, छत्व, णत्व
  विसर्ग संधि
    सत्व, षत्व, रुत्व, उत्व
    रोरि व ढ्रलोपे
@end

### संधि – परिचय
@tree संधि कार्य
संधि कार्य
  आदेश – शत्रुवदादेशः (यद्यपि)
  आगम – मित्रवदागमः (वृक्षच्छाया)
  लोप – अदर्शनं लोपः
  वर्णविकार
@end

@tree संधि कहाँ नित्य
संहिता
  नित्य
    एक पद
    धातु-उपसर्ग
    समास
  वैकल्पिक
    वाक्य – वक्ता की इच्छा
@end

- सम् + धा + कि = संधि (पुं.); "परः सन्निकर्षः संहिता"।
- "संहितैकपदे नित्या नित्या धातूपसर्गयोः। नित्या समासे वाक्ये तु सा विवक्षामपेक्षते॥" – एक पद, धातु-उपसर्ग, समास में नित्य; वाक्य में वक्ता की इच्छा पर।
- संधि कार्य: आदेश ("शत्रुवदादेशः" – यदि + अपि = यद्यपि), आगम ("मित्रवदागमः" – वृक्षच्छाया), लोप ("अदर्शनं लोपः"), वर्णविकार।
### स्वर संधि (अच् संधि) – सूत्र सहित
@tree स्वर संधि के भेद
स्वर संधि
  दीर्घ – अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः
  गुण – आद् गुणः
  वृद्धि – वृद्धिरेचि
  यण् – इको यणचि
  अयादि – एचोऽयवायावः
  पूर्वरूप – एङः पदान्तादति
  पररूप – एङि पररूपम्
  प्रकृतिभाव – ईदूदेद् द्विवचनं प्रगृह्यम्
@end

|संधि|सूत्र|उदाहरण|
|दीर्घ|अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः|हिम + आलय = हिमालय, रवि + इन्द्र = रवीन्द्र, गुरु + उपदेशः = गुरूपदेशः|
|गुण|आद् गुणः|गण + ईश = गणेश, सूर्य + उदय = सूर्योदय, देव + ऋषि = देवर्षि, तव + ऌकार = तवल्कार|
|वृद्धि|वृद्धिरेचि|सदा + एव = सदैव, जल + ओघ = जलौघ, महा + औषधि = महौषधि|
|यण्|इको यणचि|यदि + अपि = यद्यपि, इति + आदि = इत्यादि, अनु + अय = अन्वय, मधु + अरिः = मध्वरिः|
|अयादि|एचोऽयवायावः|ने + अन = नयन, भो + अन = भवन, नै + अक = नायक, पौ + अक = पावक|
|पूर्वरूप|एङः पदान्तादति|हरे + अव = हरेऽव, ते + अपि = तेऽपि|
|पररूप|एङि पररूपम्|प्र + एजते = प्रेजते, उप + ओषति = उपोषति|
|प्रकृतिभाव|ईदूदेद् द्विवचनं प्रगृह्यम्|हरी एतौ, लते एते (संधि नहीं)|
- गुण के अपवाद (वार्तिक): अक्ष + ऊहिनी = अक्षौहिणी, प्र + ऊढः = प्रौढः, स्व + ईरिणी = स्वैरिणी, सुख + ऋतः = सुखार्तः, प्र + ऋच्छति = प्रार्च्छति।
- पररूप (शकन्ध्वादि वार्तिक): शक + अन्धुः = शकन्धुः, कुल + अटा = कुलटा, मनस् + ईषा = मनीषा, पतत् + अञ्जलिः = पतञ्जलिः, सीमन् + अन्तः = सीमन्तः, मार्त + अण्डः = मार्तण्डः।
### व्यंजन संधि (हल् संधि) – सूत्र सहित
@tree व्यंजन संधि के भेद
व्यंजन संधि
  श्चुत्व – स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः
  ष्टुत्व – ष्टुना ष्टुः
  जश्त्व – झलां जशोऽन्ते
  चर्त्व – खरि च
  अनुस्वार – मोऽनुस्वारः
  परसवर्ण – अनुस्वारस्य ययि परसवर्णः
  लत्व – तोर्लि
  छत्व – शश्छोऽटि
  णत्व – रषाभ्यां नो णः समानपदे
@end

|संधि|सूत्र|उदाहरण|
|श्चुत्व|स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः|सत् + चित् = सच्चित्, सत् + जनः = सज्जनः, उत् + ज्वलः = उज्ज्वलः|
|ष्टुत्व|ष्टुना ष्टुः|तत् + टीका = तट्टीका, उत् + डयनम् = उड्डयनम्, विष् + नुः = विष्णुः|
|जश्त्व|झलां जशोऽन्ते|वाक् + ईशः = वागीशः, जगत् + ईशः = जगदीशः, षट् + आननः = षडाननः, सुप् + अन्तः = सुबन्तः|
|चर्त्व|खरि च|सद् + कारः = सत्कारः, दिग् + पालः = दिक्पालः|
|अनुस्वार|मोऽनुस्वारः|हरिम् + वन्दे = हरिं वन्दे|
|परसवर्ण|अनुस्वारस्य ययि परसवर्णः|गम् + गा = गङ्गा, शाम् + तः = शान्तः|
|लत्व|तोर्लि|तत् + लीनः = तल्लीनः, उत् + लेखः = उल्लेखः|
|छत्व|शश्छोऽटि|एतत् + शोभनम् = एतच्छोभनम्, तत् + श्रुत्वा = तच्छ्रुत्वा|
|णत्व|रषाभ्यां नो णः समानपदे|कृष् + नः = कृष्णः|
- म् के बाद स्वर हो तो मिल जाता है: रामम् + आगतः = राममागतः।
### विसर्ग संधि – सूत्र सहित
@tree विसर्ग संधि के भेद
विसर्ग संधि
  सत्व – नमस्ते
  षत्व – निष्कपटः
  रुत्व – मुनिरयम्
  उत्व – मनोरथः
  रोरि – पुनारमते
@end

|संधि|नियम|उदाहरण|
|सत्व|विसर्जनीयस्य सः – खर् परे विसर्ग → स्/श्/ष्|नमः + ते = नमस्ते, निः + चयः = निश्चयः|
|षत्व|इ/उ + विसर्ग + क, ख, प, फ → ष्|निः + कपटः = निष्कपटः, निः + फलः = निष्फलः|
|रुत्व|अ/आ से भिन्न स्वर + विसर्ग + स्वर/घोष → र्|मुनिः + अयम् = मुनिरयम्, गुरुः + जयति = गुरुर्जयति|
|उत्व|अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते, हशि च – अ + विसर्ग + अ/हश् → ओ|शिवः + अर्च्यः = शिवोऽर्च्यः, मनः + रथः = मनोरथः, सः + अवदत् = सोऽवदत्|
|रोरि व ढ्रलोपे|र् + र् → पूर्व र् लोप, पूर्व स्वर दीर्घ|पुनर् + रमते = पुनारमते, हरिर् + रम्यः = हरीरम्यः|

## समास (संस्कृत)
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree समास (संस्कृत) – माइंड मैप
समास
  परिचय
    पद विधि
    विग्रह
    5 भेद (सिद्धांतकौमुदी 6)
  अव्ययीभाव
    पूर्वपद प्रधान
    नपुं. एकवचन
  तत्पुरुष
    उत्तरपद प्रधान
    द्वितीया–सप्तमी
  कर्मधारय, द्विगु व अन्य
    कर्मधारय, द्विगु
    नञ्, प्रादि, अलुक्, उपपद
  द्वंद्व
    उभयपद प्रधान
    इतरेतर, समाहार, एकशेष
  बहुव्रीहि
    अन्यपद प्रधान
    यस्य सः
@end

### समास – परिचय
@tree समास के भेद व प्रधान पद
समास
  केवल समास
  अव्ययीभाव – पूर्वपद प्रधान
  तत्पुरुष – उत्तरपद प्रधान
    कर्मधारय
    द्विगु
  द्वंद्व – उभयपद प्रधान
  बहुव्रीहि – अन्यपद प्रधान
@end

- सम् + आस् + घञ्; "पदानां लुप्यते यत्र प्रायः स्वाः स्वाः विभक्तयः। पुनरेकपदीभावः समास उच्यते तदा॥"
- समास = पद विधि (दो पदों में), संधि = वर्ण विधि; समास का अलगाव = विग्रह, संधि का = विच्छेद।
- लौकिक विग्रह (कृष्णम् श्रितः) व अलौकिक विग्रह (सुप् प्रत्ययों में)।
- 5 भेद: केवल, अव्ययीभाव (पूर्वपद प्रधान), तत्पुरुष (उत्तरपद प्रधान; कर्मधारय, द्विगु उपभेद), द्वंद्व (उभयपद प्रधान), बहुव्रीहि (अन्यपद प्रधान); सिद्धांतकौमुदी के अनुसार 6।
- केवल समास: "विशेषसंज्ञाविनिर्मुक्तः केवलसमासः"; विधि सूत्र "सह सुपा" – भूतपूर्वः, वागर्थाविव।
### अव्ययीभाव समास
- "प्रायेण पूर्वपदार्थप्रधानोऽव्ययीभावः"; समस्त पद सदा नपुंसकलिंग एकवचन।
- 16 अर्थ (सूत्र: अव्ययं विभक्तिसमीपसमृद्धि...): अधिहरि (हरि में), उपकृष्णम् (कृष्ण के समीप), सुमद्रम् (समृद्धि), निर्मक्षिकम् (अभाव), अतिहिमम् (अत्यय), अनुविष्णु (पश्चात्), प्रत्येकम् (एक-एक), यथाशक्ति, सचक्रम् (युगपत्), ससखि (सादृश्य), सतृणम् (साकल्य)।
- "अव्ययीभावे शरत्प्रभृतिभ्यः" – उपशरदम्; "अनश्च" – उपराजम्; "नदीभिश्च" – पञ्चगङ्गम्, द्वियमुनम्।
### तत्पुरुष समास (व्यधिकरण)
@tree विभक्ति के अनुसार तत्पुरुष
तत्पुरुष
  द्वितीया – कृष्णाश्रितः
  तृतीया – हरित्रातः
  चतुर्थी – यूपदारु
  पंचमी – चोरभयम्
  षष्ठी – राजपुरुषः
  सप्तमी – अक्षशौण्डः
@end

- "प्रायेण उत्तरपदार्थप्रधानस्तत्पुरुषः"।
- द्वितीया ("द्वितीया श्रितातीतपतितगतात्यस्तप्राप्तापन्नैः"): कृष्णाश्रितः, दुःखातीतः, स्वर्गगतः, संकटापन्नः।
- तृतीया ("कर्तृकरणे कृता बहुलम्"; "पूर्वसदृशसमोनार्थ..."): हरित्रातः, नखभिन्नः, मासपूर्वम्, पितृसमः, गुडमिश्रम्।
- चतुर्थी ("चतुर्थी तदर्थार्थबलिहितसुखरक्षितैः"): यूपदारु, भूतबलिः, गोहितम्, ब्राह्मणहितम्।
- पंचमी ("पंचमी भयेन"): चोरभयम्, सिंहभीतः, सर्पभीतः; रोगमुक्तः, दूरादागतः।
- षष्ठी: राजपुरुषः, देवालयः, विद्यालयः, राष्ट्रपतिः, गंगाजलम्, हिमालयः।
- सप्तमी ("सप्तमी शौण्डैः"): अक्षशौण्डः, कार्यकुशलः, शास्त्रप्रवीणः, सभापण्डितः, कूपमण्डूकः।
### कर्मधारय, द्विगु व अन्य तत्पुरुष
@tree तत्पुरुष के अन्य रूप
तत्पुरुष के उपभेद
  कर्मधारय
    विशेषण-विशेष्य – नीलकमलम्
    उपमान-उपमेय – घनश्यामः
  द्विगु – पञ्चपात्रम्
  नञ् – असत्यम्, अनीश्वरः
  प्रादि – प्राचार्यः
  अलुक् – युधिष्ठिरः
  उपपद – कुम्भकारः
@end

- कर्मधारय ("तत्पुरुषः समानाधिकरणः कर्मधारयः"): विशेषण-विशेष्य – नीलकमलम्, महापुरुषः, पीताम्बरम्, कृष्णसर्पः; उपमान-उपमेय – घनश्यामः, विद्याधनम्, संसारसागरम्, मुखकमलम्, पुरुषव्याघ्रः, नरशार्दूलः।
- द्विगु ("संख्यापूर्वो द्विगुः"): नपुंसक एकवचन – पञ्चपात्रम्, त्रिलोकी, त्रिभुवनम्, नवरत्नम्, शताब्दी, सप्ताहः, पञ्चगव्यम्।
- नञ् ("न लोपो नञः"): न + स्वर = अन्, न + व्यंजन = अ – अब्राह्मणः, असत्यम्, अज्ञानम्, अनीश्वरः, अनश्वः, अनागतः।
- प्रादि: प्राचार्यः, प्रपितामहः, अतिमालः।
- अलुक्: युधिष्ठिरः, कर्णेजपः, मनसाकृतः, आत्मनेपदम्, परस्मैपदम्, सरसिजम्, खेचरः, वनेचरः।
- उपपद: कुम्भकारः, चर्मकारः, वेदज्ञः, धनदः, जलजः।
### द्वंद्व समास
@tree द्वंद्व के रूप
द्वंद्व (चार्थे द्वंद्वः)
  इतरेतर – रामलक्ष्मणौ
  समाहार (नपुं. एक.) – पाणिपादम्
  एकशेष (पुं. द्वि.) – पितरौ
@end

@tree द्वंद्व में पद-क्रम के नियम
पहले कौन?
  द्वंद्वे घि – ह्रस्व इ/उ वाला (हरिहरौ)
  अल्पाच्तरम् – कम स्वर वाला (शिवकेशवौ)
  भ्रातुर्ज्यायसः – बड़ा भाई (रामलक्ष्मणौ)
  अभ्यर्हितं च – अधिक पूज्य (मातापितरौ)
@end

- "चार्थे द्वंद्वः"; 4 अर्थ – समुच्चय, अन्वाचय, इतरेतर, समाहार।
- इतरेतर: रामलक्ष्मणौ, सीतारामौ, हरिहरौ, शिवकेशवौ, मातापितरौ।
- समाहार (नपुं. एकवचन): पाणिपादम्, अहिनकुलम्, अहोरात्रम्, शीतोष्णम्, दन्तौष्ठम्।
- एकशेष (पुं. द्विवचन): पितरौ (माता च पिता च), श्वशुरौ, भ्रातरौ, पुत्रौ।
- क्रम नियम: "द्वंद्वे घि" (ह्रस्व इ/उ वाले पहले – हरिहरौ), "अल्पाच्तरम्" (कम स्वर वाले पहले – शिवकेशवौ), "भ्रातुर्ज्यायसः" (बड़ा भाई पहले – रामलक्ष्मणौ), "अभ्यर्हितं च" (अधिक पूज्य पहले – मातापितरौ, राधाकृष्णौ)।
### बहुव्रीहि समास
- "अनेकमन्यपदार्थे"; विग्रह 'यस्य सः'।
|समस्त पद|विग्रह|अर्थ|
|पीताम्बरः|पीतम् अम्बरम् यस्य सः|विष्णु/कृष्ण|
|चक्रपाणिः|चक्रं पाणौ यस्य सः|विष्णु|
|वीणापाणिः|वीणा पाणौ यस्याः सा|सरस्वती|
|चन्द्रशेखरः|चन्द्रः शेखरे यस्य सः|शिव|
|गजाननः|गजस्य आननम् इव आननं यस्य सः|गणेश|
|लम्बोदरः|लम्बम् उदरम् यस्य सः|गणेश|
|दशाननः|दश आननानि यस्य सः|रावण|
|त्रिनेत्रः|त्रीणि नेत्राणि यस्य सः|शिव|
|चतुर्मुखः|चत्वारि मुखानि यस्य सः|ब्रह्मा|
|निर्धनः|निर्गतं धनं यस्मात् सः|गरीब|
! पीताम्बरम् (नपुं.) = कर्मधारय, पीताम्बरः (पुं.) = बहुव्रीहि; अपुत्रम् = नञ् तत्पुरुष, अपुत्रः = बहुव्रीहि।
= समास पहचान: 'यथा/उप' + म् = अव्ययीभाव; संख्या पहले = द्विगु; 'च' = द्वंद्व; 'यस्य सः' = बहुव्रीहि।

## प्रत्यय (संस्कृत)
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree प्रत्यय (संस्कृत) – माइंड मैप
प्रत्यय
  परिचय व 5 भेद
    कृत्, तद्धित, स्त्री, सनादि, विभक्ति
    विकरण प्रत्यय
  कृत्य (7)
    तव्य, अनीयर्, यत्… – योग्य/चाहिए
  प्रमुख कृत्
    शतृ, शानच्, क्त, क्तवतु
    क्त्वा, ल्यप्, तुमुन्
    ल्युट्, ण्वुल्, तृच्
  धातु कार्य
    आत्व, ईत्व, युक्
    सम्प्रसारण, कुत्व, वृद्धि, गुण
  तद्धित
    मतुप्, त्व/तल्
    तरप्/ईयसुन्, तमप्/इष्ठन्
  स्त्री (8)
    टाप्, डाप्, चाप्
    ङीप्, ङीष्, ङीन्
    ऊङ्, ति
@end

### प्रत्यय – परिचय व भेद
@tree प्रत्यय के 5 भेद
प्रत्यय
  कृत् – धातु से (तिङ् छोड़कर)
  तद्धित – शब्द से
  स्त्री
  सनादि (12)
  विभक्ति – सुप् 21 + तिङ् 18
@end

- प्रति + √इण् + अच्; "प्रतीयते विधीयते इति प्रत्ययः"।
- प्रकृति: धातु ("भूवादयो धातवः") व प्रातिपदिक ("अर्थवदधातुरप्रत्ययः प्रातिपदिकम्")।
- 5 भेद: कृत् (धातु से, तिङ् छोड़कर – "कृदतिङ्"), तद्धित (शब्द से), स्त्री, सनादि (12), विभक्ति (सुप् 21 + तिङ् 18)।
- विकरण प्रत्यय (धातु व तिङ् के बीच): शप् (भवति), श्यन्, श्नु, श्ना (क्रीणाति), उ (करोति)।
### कृत्य प्रत्यय (7)
@tree 7 कृत्य प्रत्यय
कृत्य (योग्य/चाहिए)
  तव्य
  तव्यत्
  अनीयर्
  केलिमर्
  यत्
  ण्यत्
  क्यप्
@end

- तव्य, तव्यत्, अनीयर्, केलिमर्, यत्, ण्यत्, क्यप् – 'योग्य/चाहिए' अर्थ; भाव व कर्म वाच्य में ("तयोरेव कृत्यक्तखलर्थाः")।
- उदाहरण: कर्तव्यः, पठितव्यः, पठनीयः, देयः, कार्यम्; "रामेण पत्रं लेखनीयम्"।
### प्रमुख कृत् प्रत्यय
@tree काल/अर्थ के अनुसार कृत् प्रत्यय
कृत् प्रत्यय
  वर्तमान
    शतृ – परस्मैपदी (पठन्)
    शानच् – आत्मनेपदी (सेवमानः)
  भूतकाल (निष्ठा)
    क्त – कर्म/भाव वाच्य (गतः)
    क्तवतु – कर्तृवाच्य (गतवान्)
  पूर्वकालिक
    क्त्वा – बिना उपसर्ग (पठित्वा)
    ल्यप् – उपसर्ग सहित (प्रणम्य)
  तुमुन् – के लिए (पठितुम्)
  ल्युट् – भाव (पठनम्)
  कर्ता
    ण्वुल् – पाठकः
    तृच् – कर्ता, नेता
@end

|प्रत्यय|अर्थ|शेष|उदाहरण|
|शतृ|वर्तमान (परस्मैपदी)|अत्|पठन्, गच्छन्, कुर्वन्; स्त्री. पठन्ती|
|शानच्|वर्तमान (आत्मनेपदी)|आन/मान|सेवमानः, लभमानः|
|क्त|भूतकाल (कर्म/भाव वाच्य)|त|गतः, कृतः, दृष्टः, पीतः|
|क्तवतु|भूतकाल (कर्तृवाच्य)|तवत्|गतवान्, पठितवान्, पठितवती|
|क्त्वा|पूर्वकालिक (बिना उपसर्ग)|त्वा|पठित्वा, गत्वा, कृत्वा, दृष्ट्वा, ज्ञात्वा|
|ल्यप्|पूर्वकालिक (उपसर्ग सहित)|य|प्रणम्य, आनीय, विज्ञाय, आगत्य, प्राप्य|
|तुमुन्|के लिए|तुम्|पठितुम्, गन्तुम्, द्रष्टुम्, पातुम्|
|ल्युट्|भाव (नपुं.)|अन|पठनम्, गमनम्, दर्शनम्, श्रवणम्|
|ण्वुल्|कर्ता|अक|पाठकः, लेखकः, गायकः, नायकः; स्त्री. पाठिका|
|तृच्|कर्ता|तृ|कर्ता, नेता, दाता|
- शतृ-शानच्: "लटः शतृशानचौ..."; क्त-क्तवतु = निष्ठा ("क्तक्तवतू निष्ठा"); क्त्वा: "समानकर्तृकयोः पूर्वकाले"; ल्यप्: "समासेऽनञ्पूर्वे क्त्वो ल्यप्"।
- क्त्वा व ल्यप् से बने शब्द अव्यय होते हैं।
- वाक्य: "सः पठितुं विद्यालयं गच्छति"; "रामेण पाठः पठितः" (क्त, कर्मवाच्य); "रामः पाठं पठितवान्" (क्तवतु)।
### कृत् प्रत्ययों में धातु कार्य
- आत्व ("आदेच उपदेशेऽशिति"), ईत्व ("ईद्यति" – पीयते, दीयते)।
- युक् आगम ("आतो युक् चिण्कृतोः"): दायकः, पायकः, गायकः।
- सम्प्रसारण: वच् → उक्तः, स्वप् → सुप्तः, यज् → इष्टवान्, प्रच्छ् → पृष्टः।
- कुत्व ("चजोः कु घिण्ण्यतोः"): वाक्यम्, भोग्यम्, रोगः।
- वृद्धि: नायकः, कार्यम्, पाठः; गुण: भवितव्यम्, करणीयम्, नेता।
- सनादि 12 (सन्, क्यच्, काम्यच्, यङ्, णिच् आदि): पिपठिषति, बोभूयते, पुत्रकाम्यति।
- सार्वधातुक ("तिङ्शित्सार्वधातुकम्") व आर्धधातुक ("आर्धधातुकं शेषः")।
- ण्यत् ("ऋहलोर्ण्यत्"): कार्यम्, वाद्यम्, ग्राह्यम्; तव्यत्/अनीयर् ("तव्यत्तव्यानीयरः"): कर्तव्यः, करणीयः, पठनीयः, दर्शनीयः, श्रवणीयः।
- घञ् (भाव, पुं.): पाठः, पाकः, रोगः, भोगः, जयः; क्तिन् (भाव, स्त्री.): गतिः।
### तद्धित प्रत्यय
@flow तुलना (लघु शब्द से)
लघु
दो में तुलना: लघुतरः (तरप्) / लघीयान् (ईयसुन्)
बहुतों में: लघुतमः (तमप्) / श्रेष्ठः (इष्ठन्)
@end

- "तेभ्यः हितम् इति तद्धितम्"।
- मतुप् ("तदस्यास्त्यस्मिन्निति मतुप्"): धनवान्, गुणवान्, बलवान्, बुद्धिमान्, श्रीमान्, विद्यावान्; स्त्री. धनवती।
- तल् (स्त्री.) व त्व (नपुं.) – "तस्य भावस्त्वतलौ": सुंदरता, लघुता, देवत्वम्, मनुष्यत्वम्।
- तरप्/ईयसुन् (दो में तुलना): लघुतरः, लघीयान्; तमप्/इष्ठन् (बहुतों में): लघुतमः, श्रेष्ठः।
### स्त्री प्रत्यय (8)
|प्रत्यय|शेष|उदाहरण|
|टाप्|आ|बालिका, अजा, शिक्षिका|
|डाप्|आ|सीमा|
|चाप्|आ|सूर्या|
|ङीप्|ई|नदी, गौरी, त्रिलोकी|
|ङीष्|ई|नर्तकी|
|ङीन्|ई|ब्राह्मणी|
|ऊङ्|ऊ|श्वश्रूः|
|ति|इ|युवतिः|

## कारक एवं विभक्ति (संस्कृत)
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree कारक एवं विभक्ति (संस्कृत) – माइंड मैप
कारक एवं विभक्ति
  कारक (6)
    कर्ता, कर्म, करण
    सम्प्रदान, अपादान, अधिकरण
    संबंध व संबोधन कारक नहीं
  प्रथमा व द्वितीया
    स्वतंत्रः कर्ता
    द्विकर्मक, अधिशीङ्स्थासां कर्म
    उपपद द्वितीया
  तृतीया
    सहयुक्तेऽप्रधाने
    येनाङ्गविकारः
    हेतौ
  चतुर्थी
    रुच्यर्थानां
    क्रुध-द्रुह
    नमः-स्वस्ति
  पंचमी
    भीत्रार्थानां
    जनिकर्तुः प्रकृतिः
    अलगाव

    संबंध

    निर्धारण

    कर्तृ, कर्म, भाव
  अनुवाद
    हिन्दी → संस्कृत
@end

### कारक – परिचय
@tree 6 कारक व विभक्ति
कारक (6)
  कर्ता – प्रथमा
  कर्म – द्वितीया
  करण – तृतीया
  सम्प्रदान – चतुर्थी
  अपादान – पंचमी
  अधिकरण – सप्तमी
@end

- "क्रियां करोति इति कारकम्"; "क्रियाजनकत्वं कारकत्वम्"।
- संस्कृत में 6 कारक: "कर्ता कर्म च करणं च सम्प्रदानं तथैव च। अपादानाधिकरणे इत्याहुः कारकाणि षट्॥"
- संबंध (षष्ठी) व संबोधन कारक नहीं माने जाते (क्रिया से सीधा संबंध नहीं)।
### प्रथमा व द्वितीया विभक्ति
- "स्वतंत्रः कर्ता"; "प्रातिपदिकार्थलिङ्गपरिमाणवचनमात्रे प्रथमा"; "सम्बोधने च" (हे राम!); "उक्ते कर्तरि प्रथमा"।
- द्विकर्मक धातुएँ: दुह् (गोपालः धेनुं दुग्धं दोग्धि), याच् (बलिं वसुधां याचते), पच्, प्रच्छ् (गुरुः शिष्यं प्रश्नं पृच्छति), ब्रू।
- "अधिशीङ्स्थासां कर्म": राजा सिंहासनम् अधितिष्ठति, हरिः वैकुण्ठम् अधिशेते।
- "अन्तरान्तरेण युक्ते": अन्तरा ग्रामं नदी; भवन्तम् अन्तरेण न जीवामि।
- उपपद द्वितीया: अभितः, परितः (विद्यालयं परितः वृक्षाः), समया, निकषा, प्रति, धिक् (धिक् मूर्खम्), उभयतः, सर्वतः।
- "कालाध्वनोरत्यन्तसंयोगे": मासम् अधीते, क्रोशम् अधीते।
### तृतीया विभक्ति
- "सहयुक्तेऽप्रधाने": पुत्रेण सह आगतः पिता; रामः जानक्या साकं गच्छति।
- "येनाङ्गविकारः": सः पादेन खञ्जः; "इत्थंभूतलक्षणे": जटाभिः तापसः।
- "हेतौ": अध्ययनेन वसति; "प्रकृत्यादिभ्य उपसंख्यानम्": शिशुः स्वभावेन चञ्चलः।
- पृथक्/विना/नाना के योग में तृतीया: जलेन विना कमलं न जीवति; अलम् कोलाहलेन।
### चतुर्थी विभक्ति
- "रुच्यर्थानां प्रीयमाणः": हरये रोचते भक्तिः।
- "क्रुधद्रुहेर्ष्यासूयार्थानां यं प्रति कोपः": असुरः हरये क्रुध्यति।
- "स्पृहेरीप्सितः": सा पुष्पेभ्यः स्पृहयति।
- "नमःस्वस्तिस्वाहास्वधालंवषड्योगाच्च": गणेशाय नमः।
- "धारेरुत्तमर्णः": रामः नरेशाय शतं धारयति; "तादर्थ्ये चतुर्थी": बालकः मोदकाय रोदिति।
- दान के योग में: राजा ब्राह्मणाय गां ददाति।
### पंचमी विभक्ति
- "भीत्रार्थानां भयहेतुः": मोहनः चोरात् बिभेति।
- "जुगुप्साविरामप्रमादार्थानामुपसंख्यानम्": पापात् जुगुप्सते।
- "आख्यातोपयोगे": छात्रः अध्यापकात् अधीते।
- "जनिकर्तुः प्रकृतिः": ब्रह्मणः प्रजाः प्रजायन्ते; "भुवः प्रभवः": हिमवतः गङ्गा प्रभवति।
- "वारणार्थानामीप्सितः": यवेभ्यः गां वारयति।
- "अन्यारादितरर्तेदिक्शब्द...": अन्यः कृष्णात्।
- अलगाव: वृक्षात् पत्रं पतति।
### षष्ठी व सप्तमी विभक्ति
@tree सप्तमी – आधार के 3 प्रकार
आधार
  औपश्लेषिक – भौतिक
  वैषयिक – बौद्धिक
  अभिव्यापक – सर्वस्मिन् आत्मा
@end

- षष्ठी संबंध: स्व-स्वामी (राज्ञः पुरुषः), जन्य-जनक (दशरथस्य पुत्रः), कार्य-कारण (मृत्तिकायाः घटः)।
- "षष्ठी हेतुप्रयोगे": अन्नस्य हेतोः वसति; "कर्तृकर्मणोः कृति": कृष्णस्य कृतिः।
- सप्तमी आधार के 3 प्रकार: औपश्लेषिक (भौतिक), वैषयिक (बौद्धिक), अभिव्यापक (सर्वस्मिन् आत्मा)।
- "यतश्च निर्धारणम्": कविषु कालिदासः श्रेष्ठः (षष्ठी/सप्तमी)।
- "साध्वसाधुप्रयोगे च": कृष्णः मातरि साधुः।
- "यस्य च भावेन भावलक्षणम्": गोषु दुह्यमानासु सः अगच्छत्।
### वाच्य (संस्कृत)
@flow कर्तृवाच्य → कर्मवाच्य
रामः पुस्तकं पठति (कर्ता प्रथमा, कर्म द्वितीया)
कर्ता → तृतीया (रामेण)
कर्म → प्रथमा (पुस्तकम्)
क्रिया कर्म के अनुसार: रामेण पुस्तकं पठ्यते
@end

|वाच्य|प्रधान|कर्ता|कर्म|क्रिया|
|कर्तृवाच्य|कर्ता|प्रथमा|द्वितीया|कर्ता के अनुसार|
|कर्मवाच्य|कर्म|तृतीया|प्रथमा|कर्म के अनुसार|
|भाववाच्य|भाव|तृतीया|–|नपुं. एकवचन|
- रामः पुस्तकं पठति → रामेण पुस्तकं पठ्यते; अहं जलं पिबामि → मया जलं पीयते।
### हिन्दी से संस्कृत अनुवाद
|हिन्दी|संस्कृत|
|मैं विद्यालय जाता हूँ।|अहं विद्यालयं गच्छामि।|
|हम दोनों पढ़ते हैं।|आवां पठावः।|
|तुम सब लिखते हो।|यूयं लिखथ।|
|वे दोनों हँसते हैं।|तौ हसतः।|
|माँ बालक को फल देती है।|माता बालकाय फलं ददाति।|
|राम सीता के साथ जाता है।|रामः सीतया सह गच्छति।|
|पेड़ से पत्ता गिरता है।|वृक्षात् पत्रं पतति।|
|गंगा हिमालय से निकलती है।|गङ्गा हिमालयात् प्रभवति।|
|बालक पलंग पर सोता है।|बालकः पर्यङ्के शेते।|
|कवियों में कालिदास श्रेष्ठ हैं।|कविषु कालिदासः श्रेष्ठः।|

## व्यावहारिक संस्कृत
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree व्यावहारिक संस्कृत – माइंड मैप
व्यावहारिक संस्कृत
  घर व परिवार
    गृहम्, कपाटः, गवाक्षः
    भ्राता, भगिनी, मातुलः
  पशु-पक्षी
    गौः, अश्वः, गजः
    काकः, मयूरः, शुकः
  शरीर व वस्तुएँ
    शिरः, नेत्रम्, हस्तः
    लेखनी, छत्रम्, घटी
  संख्याएँ
    1-4 तीनों लिंगों में अलग
    5-10 समान
    ऊन = एक कम
  दिशा, समय, रंग, फल-सब्ज़ी
    प्राची, प्रतीची, उदीची, दक्षिणा
    श्वेतः, रक्तः, पीतः
@end

### संस्कृत संख्याएँ
|संख्या|पुं.|स्त्री.|नपुं.|
|1|एकः|एका|एकम्|
|2|द्वौ|द्वे|द्वे|
|3|त्रयः|तिस्रः|त्रीणि|
|4|चत्वारः|चतस्रः|चत्वारि|
- 5-10: पञ्च, षट्, सप्त, अष्ट, नव, दश (तीनों लिंगों में समान)।
- 11-20: एकादश, द्वादश, त्रयोदश, चतुर्दश, पञ्चदश, षोडश, सप्तदश, अष्टादश, नवदश/ऊनविंशतिः, विंशतिः।
- 29 ऊनत्रिंशत्, 30 त्रिंशत्, 39 ऊनचत्वारिंशत्, 40 चत्वारिंशत्, 50 पञ्चाशत्, 60 षष्टिः, 70 सप्ततिः, 80 अशीतिः, 90 नवतिः, 99 नवनवतिः/ऊनशतम्, 100 शतम्।
- 1000 सहस्रम्, 1 लाख लक्षम्, 1 करोड़ कोटिः।
- क्रमवाचक: प्रथमः, द्वितीयः, तृतीयः, चतुर्थः, पञ्चमः, षष्ठः, सप्तमः, अष्टमः, नवमः, दशमः।
= ट्रिक: 'ऊन' = एक कम (ऊनविंशतिः = 19)।
### दिशाएँ, समय, रंग, फल-सब्ज़ियाँ
@tree दिशाएँ
दिशा
  प्राची – पूर्व
  प्रतीची – पश्चिम
  उदीची – उत्तर
  दक्षिणा – दक्षिण
  उपरि – ऊपर
  अधः – नीचे
@end

- दिशाएँ: प्राची (पूर्व), प्रतीची (पश्चिम), उदीची (उत्तर), दक्षिणा (दक्षिण); उपरि (ऊपर), अधः (नीचे), वामतः (बाएँ)।
- समय: प्रातः, सायम्, मध्याह्नः, अर्धरात्रिः, अद्य, श्वः, ह्यः, सप्ताहः, मासः, होरा (घंटा), क्षणः।
- रंग: श्वेतः (सफेद), कृष्णः (काला), रक्तः (लाल), हरितः (हरा), पीतः (पीला), नीलः, कपिशः (भूरा), पाटलः (गुलाबी)।
- फल: आम्रम्, कदलीफलम् (केला), नारङ्गम् (संतरा), द्राक्षा (अंगूर), दाडिमम् (अनार), नारिकेलम्, जम्बीरम् (नींबू), बदरीफलम् (बेर), खर्जूरम्।
- सब्ज़ियाँ: आलुकम्, वृन्ताकम् (बैंगन), अलाबुः (लौकी), कूष्माण्डः (कद्दू), गृञ्जनम् (गाजर), मूलकम्, पलाण्डुः (प्याज़), लशुनम्, आर्द्रकम् (अदरक), पालक्यम्।
- वाक्य: सूर्यः प्राच्याः उदेति; मयूरः नृत्यति; कोकिला मधुरं गायति; गौः दुग्धं ददाति।

## संस्कृत साहित्य – कवि एवं रचनाएँ
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree संस्कृत साहित्य – कवि एवं रचनाएँ – माइंड मैप
संस्कृत साहित्य
  कालिदास
    कविकुलगुरु
    7 रचनाएँ
    उपमा कालिदासस्य
  अन्य कवि
    बाणभट्ट, भवभूति, भारवि, माघ
    श्रीहर्ष, दण्डी, जयदेव…
  त्रयी व महाग्रंथ
    बृहत्त्रयी, लघुत्रयी, पाषाणत्रयी
    रामायण, महाभारत, गीता
  सूक्तियाँ
    सत्यमेव जयते
    वसुधैव कुटुम्बकम्
@end

### महाकवि कालिदास
@tree कालिदास की 7 रचनाएँ
कालिदास
  महाकाव्य
    कुमारसम्भवम् (17 सर्ग)
    रघुवंशम् (19 सर्ग)
  खंडकाव्य
    ऋतुसंहारम्
    मेघदूतम्
  नाटक
    मालविकाग्निमित्रम्
    विक्रमोर्वशीयम्
    अभिज्ञानशाकुन्तलम्
@end

- चौथी शताब्दी ई. (गुप्त काल); राजाश्रय चंद्रगुप्त विक्रमादित्य (उज्जयिनी); उपाधि 'कविकुलगुरु'; "उपमा कालिदासस्य"।
- 7 रचनाएँ – ट्रिक "ऋतु कुमार का मालविका, मेघ रघु का ज्ञान":
|रचना|प्रकार|विषय|
|ऋतुसंहारम्|खंडकाव्य|छह ऋतुओं का वर्णन|
|कुमारसम्भवम्|महाकाव्य (17 सर्ग)|कार्तिकेय का जन्म|
|मालविकाग्निमित्रम्|नाटक|अग्निमित्र-मालविका|
|विक्रमोर्वशीयम्|नाटक|पुरुरवा-उर्वशी|
|मेघदूतम्|खंडकाव्य|यक्ष का संदेश (पूर्वमेघ, उत्तरमेघ)|
|रघुवंशम्|महाकाव्य (19 सर्ग)|रघुकुल की कथा|
|अभिज्ञानशाकुन्तलम्|नाटक|शकुंतला-दुष्यंत|
- रघुवंश का मंगलश्लोक: "वागर्थाविव सम्पृक्तौ वागर्थप्रतिपत्तये। जगतः पितरौ वन्दे पार्वतीपरमेश्वरौ॥"
### अन्य संस्कृत कवि
@timeline शताब्दी के अनुसार कवि
4थी शती | शूद्रक (कालिदास भी चौथी शताब्दी)
6ठी शती | भारवि, अमरसिंह
7वीं शती | बाणभट्ट, भर्तृहरि, हर्षवर्धन, दण्डी, विशाखदत्त
8वीं शती | भवभूति, माघ
12वीं शती | श्रीहर्ष, जयदेव, वरदराज
17वीं शती | भट्टोजिदीक्षित
@end

|कवि|समय|रचनाएँ|विशेष|
|बाणभट्ट|7वीं शती|हर्षचरितम्, कादम्बरी, चण्डीशतकम्|हर्षवर्धन के आश्रित; गद्य के श्रेष्ठ लेखक|
|भवभूति|8वीं शती|मालतीमाधवम्, महावीरचरितम्, उत्तररामचरितम्|करुण रस के कवि – "एको रसः करुण एव"|
|भारवि|6वीं शती|किरातार्जुनीयम् (18 सर्ग)|"भारवेः अर्थगौरवम्"; नारिकेलफल समान|
|भर्तृहरि|7वीं शती|शतकत्रयी (शृंगार, वैराग्य, नीति), वाक्यपदीय|–|
|हर्षवर्धन|7वीं शती|प्रियदर्शिका, रत्नावली, नागानंद|ट्रिक "की प्रिय रत्ना"|
|श्रीहर्ष|12वीं शती|नैषधीयचरितम् (22 सर्ग)|नल-दमयंती|
|माघ|8वीं शती|शिशुपालवधम् (20 सर्ग)|"माघे सन्ति त्रयो गुणाः"|
|दण्डी|7वीं शती|दशकुमारचरितम्, काव्यादर्श|"दण्डिनः पदलालित्यम्"|
|जयदेव|12वीं शती|गीतगोविन्दम्|राधा-कृष्ण प्रेमलीला|
|विशाखदत्त|7वीं शती|मुद्राराक्षसम्|चाणक्य-चंद्रगुप्त|
|शूद्रक|4थी शती|मृच्छकटिकम्|चारुदत्त-वसंतसेना|
|अमरसिंह|6ठी शती|अमरकोश (नामलिङ्गानुशासन)|शब्दकोश|
|वरदराज|12वीं शती|लघुसिद्धान्तकौमुदी|43 प्रत्याहार|
|भट्टोजिदीक्षित|17वीं शती|सिद्धान्तकौमुदी|–|
|अम्बिकादत्त व्यास|–|शिवराजविजयः|ऐतिहासिक उपन्यास|
- पतंजलि: महाभाष्य व योगसूत्र।
### महाकाव्य त्रयी, रामायण, महाभारत
@tree काव्य त्रयी
त्रयी
  बृहत्त्रयी
    किरातार्जुनीयम् – भारवि (18)
    शिशुपालवधम् – माघ (20)
    नैषधीयचरितम् – श्रीहर्ष (22)
  लघुत्रयी (कालिदास)
    कुमारसम्भवम्
    रघुवंशम्
    मेघदूतम्
  पाषाणत्रयी – किरातार्जुनीयम् के प्रथम 3 सर्ग
@end

@flow रामायण के 7 कांड (क्रम से)
बाल
अयोध्या
अरण्य
किष्किंधा
सुंदर
लंका (युद्ध)
उत्तर
@end

- बृहत्त्रयी (ट्रिक "भा-कि, मा-शि, श्री-नै"): किरातार्जुनीयम् (भारवि, 18), शिशुपालवधम् (माघ, 20), नैषधीयचरितम् (श्रीहर्ष, 22)।
- लघुत्रयी (कालिदास): कुमारसम्भवम्, रघुवंशम्, मेघदूतम्।
- पाषाणत्रयी: किरातार्जुनीयम् के प्रथम तीन सर्ग।
- रामायण: वाल्मीकि (आदिकवि), 24,000 श्लोक ('चतुर्विंशतिसाहस्री संहिता'), 7 कांड – बाल, अयोध्या, अरण्य, किष्किंधा, सुंदर, लंका (युद्ध), उत्तर।
- महाभारत: वेदव्यास (कृष्ण द्वैपायन), 1,00,000 श्लोक ('शतसाहस्री संहिता', जयसंहिता), 18 पर्व।
- भगवद्गीता: 18 अध्याय, 700 श्लोक, भीष्म पर्व से।
### प्रसिद्ध सूक्तियाँ
|सूक्ति|स्रोत|
|धर्मो रक्षति रक्षितः|महाभारत|
|अहं ब्रह्मास्मि|उपनिषद् (बृहदारण्यक)|
|तत्त्वमसि|छान्दोग्य उपनिषद्|
|सत्यमेव जयते|मुण्डक उपनिषद्|
|वसुधैव कुटुम्बकम्|महोपनिषद्|
|अतिथिदेवो भव|तैत्तिरीय उपनिषद्|

# English
## The Sentence and Its Parts
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
The Sentence and Its Parts
  Subject and Predicate
    Subject: ask "Who?/What?" before verb
    Predicate: always contains the verb
  Main Verb vs Helping Verb
    Helping verb: tense or mood
    'is'/'have' alone = MAIN verb
    Transitive (object) / Intransitive (no object)
    Linking verbs → Subject Complement
  Object vs Complement
    Object: different entity (teaches English)
    Complement: same entity (is a teacher)
  Parts of Speech, Phrase and Clause
    8 parts of speech
    Phrase: no subject + verb
    Clause: has subject + verb
  Types of Sentences
    Structure: Simple, Compound, Complex
    Function: Assertive, Interrogative, Imperative
    Function: Exclamatory, Optative
@end

### Subject and Predicate
- A sentence = group of words making complete sense; two parts: Subject and Predicate.
- Subject: ask "Who?/What?" before the verb – "My brother will return tomorrow."
- Predicate: tells something about the subject; always contains the verb.
### Main Verb vs Helping Verb
@tree Kinds of verbs
Verb
  By role
    Main verb – primary action/state
    Helping (auxiliary) – tense or mood
  By object
    Transitive – needs object (kicked the football)
    Intransitive – no object (Birds fly)
  Linking verb
    be, seem, look, appear, become
    feel, taste, smell
    takes Subject Complement
@end

- Main verb shows primary action/state; helping (auxiliary) verb expresses tense or mood – "She has written a letter."
! Trap: In "He is a teacher" / "I have a pen", 'is' and 'have' are MAIN verbs (no other verb to help).
- Transitive: needs an object (kicked the football); Intransitive: no object (Birds fly; He runs fast – 'fast' is adverb).
- Linking verbs (be, seem, look, appear, become, feel, taste, smell) take a Subject Complement – "The soup tastes delicious."
### Object vs Complement
- Object receives the action and is a different entity: "Sagar teaches English."
- Complement describes/renames the subject (same entity): "Sagar is a teacher."
### Parts of Speech, Phrase and Clause
@tree Phrase vs Clause
Group of words
  Phrase – no subject + verb
    in the garden
  Clause – has subject + verb
    Main clause – stands alone
    Subordinate clause – because, if, when, that, which
@end

- 8 parts of speech: Noun, Pronoun, Adjective, Verb, Adverb, Preposition, Conjunction, Interjection.
- Phrase: no subject + verb (in the garden); Clause: has subject + verb (because he was ill).
- Main clause stands alone; subordinate clause starts with because, if, when, that, which.
### Types of Sentences
@tree Types of sentences
Sentence
  By Structure
    Simple – one main clause
    Compound – FANBOYS
    Complex – main + subordinate
  By Function
    Assertive – He is a good boy.
    Interrogative – Are you reading this book?
    Imperative – Please help me.
    Exclamatory – What a beautiful smile she has!
    Optative (wish/prayer) – May you live long!
@end

|Basis|Type|Example|
|Structure|Simple (one main clause)|Pawan has read this book.|
|Structure|Compound (FANBOYS)|Radhika lives in Delhi, but her parents live in UP.|
|Structure|Complex (main + subordinate)|I will help him if he asks me.|
|Function|Assertive|He is a good boy.|
|Function|Interrogative|Are you reading this book?|
|Function|Imperative|Please help me.|
|Function|Exclamatory|What a beautiful smile she has!|
|Function|Optative (wish/prayer)|May you live long!|

## The Adverb
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
The Adverb
  Types of Adverbs
    Modifies verb, adjective, adverb
    Manner, Place, Time, Frequency
    Degree, Interrogative
  Position of Adverbs
    MPT: Manner → Place → Time
    PMT for verbs of movement
    Never between verb and direct object
    Specific → general (at 10 PM on Monday)
  Confusing Adverb Pairs
    hard (effort) / hardly (almost not)
    late / lately (recently)
    very (positive) / too (excess)
    too much + noun / much too + adjective
  Adverb Traps
    -ly adjectives → in a friendly manner
    no 'fastly', 'straightly'
    too...to = so...that...cannot
    no preposition before today, here, abroad
@end

### Types of Adverbs
@tree Types of adverbs
Adverb
  Manner (how) – beautifully, well
  Place (where) – here, abroad
  Time (when) – now, soon, ago
  Frequency (how often) – always, never, daily
  Degree (how much) – very, too, enough, rather, hardly
  Interrogative – why, when, where, how
@end

- Adverb modifies a verb, adjective or another adverb – He runs very fast.
- Manner (how – beautifully, well), Place (where – here, abroad), Time (when – now, soon, ago), Frequency (how often – always, never, daily), Degree (how much – very, too, enough, rather, hardly), Interrogative (why, when, where, how).
### Position of Adverbs
@flow MPT rule – order of adverbs
Manner – beautifully
Place – in the party
Time – last week
@end

@tree Where frequency adverbs go
Indefinite frequency adverb
  Before main verb – He always comes
  After helping verb – He has always helped
  After 'be' – I am always happy
@end

- MPT rule: Manner → Place → Time – "He sang beautifully in the party last week."
- PMT for verbs of movement (go, come, arrive, reach): "He came into the room hurriedly."
- Indefinite frequency adverbs: before main verb (He always comes), after helping verb (He has always helped), after 'be' (I am always happy).
- Never put an adverb between verb and direct object: "I read the book carefully" (not "read carefully the book").
- Multiple time/place adverbs: specific → general – "at 10 PM on Monday in June".
### Confusing Adverb Pairs
|Pair|Usage|
|Hard / Hardly|hard = with effort; hardly = almost not ("He hardly works")|
|Late / Lately|late = not on time; lately = recently (with Present Perfect)|
|High / Highly|high = at a height; highly = very much ("highly respected")|
|Free / Freely|free = without cost; freely = without restriction|
|Fairly / Rather|fairly + pleasant adjectives; rather + unpleasant ones|
|Very / Too|very = high degree; too = excess, negative ("too hot to drink")|
|Very / Much|very + positive degree & -ing; much + comparative & -ed ("much interested")|
|Too much / Much too|too much + noun; much too + adjective|
|Loud / Loudly / Aloud|loudly = noisily; aloud = audibly ("read aloud")|
|Dead / Deadly|dead tired = completely; deadly = fatal|
### Adverb Traps
- -ly adjectives (friendly, cowardly, lovely, scholarly) → use "in a friendly manner".
- Same form adj/adv: fast, straight, hard, late, daily – no 'fastly', 'straightly'.
- too...to = so...that...cannot – "He is too weak to walk."
- so + adjective + that; such + a + adjective + noun + that ("such a smart boy"; or "so smart a boy").
- No preposition before today, tomorrow, yesterday, here, abroad – "He came here yesterday", "She lives abroad".

## Inversion
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Inversion
  Partial Inversion
    Helping verb + Subject + Main verb
    Negative adverbs: Seldom, Rarely, Never, Little
    No sooner ... than
    Hardly/Scarcely ... when/before
    Only + adverbial → inversion
    Not only at the start → inversion
    Under no circumstances, Not until
  Full Inversion
    Main verb + Subject
    After Here, There, Along, Outside, Under
    Intransitive verbs of being/movement
    Not if subject is a pronoun (Here he comes)
@end

### Partial Inversion (Helping verb + Subject + Main verb)
@flow Partial inversion order
Negative adverb (Seldom)
Helping verb (does)
Subject (he)
Main verb (visit his parents)
@end

@tree When 'Only' / 'Not only' inverts
Only / Not only
  Only + adverbial → inversion
    Only then did I understand
  Only + subject → no inversion
    Only Ram can solve this
  Not only at the start → inversion
  Not only in the middle → no inversion
@end

- Starting with negative adverbs: Seldom, Rarely, Scarcely, Hardly, Barely, Never, Nowhere, Little – "Seldom does he visit his parents."
- No sooner did + S + V1 ... than; Hardly/Scarcely had + S + V3 ... when/before.
! No sooner ... than (never 'then' or 'when'); Hardly ... when (never 'than').
- Only + adverbial: "Only then did I understand", "Only in this way can you succeed"; but "Only Ram can solve this" – no inversion (only modifies subject).
- Not only at the start → inversion: "Not only did she object..."; in the middle → no inversion.
- At no cost, Under no circumstances, Not until, On no account → inversion: "Under no circumstances should you open that door."
### Full Inversion (Main verb + Subject)
@flow Full inversion order
Adverb of place (Here)
Main verb (comes)
Subject (the bus)
@end

- After adverbs of place (Here, There, Along, Outside, Under): "Here comes the bus", "Along the northern boundary lie the Himalayas."
- Used with intransitive verbs of being/movement.
! No full inversion if the subject is a pronoun ("Here he comes", not "Here comes he") or the verb is transitive.

## Conjunctions
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Conjunctions
  Coordinating and Subordinating
    FANBOYS join equal elements
    Inversion after 'nor'
    Subordinating: time, reason, condition...
  Correlative Pairs
    Neither ... nor
    Not only ... but also
    Though ... yet
    No sooner ... than; Hardly ... when
    Lest ... should
    Parallelism: balanced elements
  Conjunction Rules
    The reason why ... was that
    Unless / Until – never add 'not'
    If = condition; Whether = choice/doubt
    No double conjunctions (as...so)
@end

### Coordinating and Subordinating
@tree Kinds of conjunctions
Conjunction
  Coordinating – FANBOYS
    For, And, Nor, But, Or, Yet, So
  Subordinating
    Time – when, while, since, until, as soon as
    Reason – because, since, as
    Condition – if, unless, provided that
    Contrast – though, although
    Purpose – so that, lest
    Result – so...that
    Comparison – than, as...as
    Manner – as if
@end

- Coordinating = FANBOYS (For, And, Nor, But, Or, Yet, So) – join equal elements; inversion after 'nor' ("nor does he drink").
- Subordinating: Time (when, while, since, until, as soon as), Reason (because, since, as), Condition (if, unless, provided that), Contrast (though, although), Purpose (so that, lest), Result (so...that), Comparison (than, as...as), Manner (as if).
### Correlative Pairs
|Correct pair|Common wrong pair|
|Either ... or|Either ... and|
|Neither ... nor|Neither ... or|
|Both ... and|Both ... as well as|
|Not only ... but also|Not only ... and also|
|Whether ... or|Whether ... and|
|Though ... yet|Though ... but|
|No sooner ... than|No sooner ... when/then|
|Hardly/Scarcely ... when|Hardly ... than|
|Lest ... should|Lest ... may/will/not|
|Other ... than|Other ... but|
|Between ... and|Between ... to|
|From ... to|From ... and|
- Parallelism: "He bought not only a book but also a pen" (balanced elements).
### Conjunction Rules
- "The reason why ... was that ..." (not 'because').
- Unless (condition) and Until (time) are already negative – never add 'not'.
- If = condition; Whether = choice/doubt ("I doubt whether he is honest").
- Lest is followed by 'should' or V1 – "Work hard lest you should fail."
- Avoid double conjunctions: "As he was ill, he did not come" (not "as...so"); "Although it was raining, I went" (not "although...but").

## Non-Finite Verbs
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Non-Finite Verbs
  Finite vs Non-Finite
    Finite: changes with subject/tense
    Non-finite: infinitive, gerund, participle
  The Infinitive
    to + V1 (noun or purpose)
    Bare infinitive: modals, let, make
    Avoid split infinitive
    Passive: to be + V3
  The Gerund
    V1 + ing used as a noun
    After prepositions, possessives
    look forward to, be used to + V-ing
    After enjoy, avoid, admit, deny
  The Participle
    Present (V-ing): a crying baby
    Past (V3): a broken chair
    Perfect: Having finished...
    Dangling participle error
  Gerund vs Infinitive
    Stop, Remember, Forget, Try, Regret
@end

### Finite vs Non-Finite
@tree Finite vs non-finite
Verb
  Finite – changes with subject/tense
    He plays / They play
  Non-finite – does not change
    Infinitive – to + V1
    Gerund – V1 + ing as noun
    Participle
      Present (V-ing, active)
      Past (V3, passive)
      Perfect (Having + V3)
@end

- Finite verbs change with subject/tense (He plays/They play); non-finite verbs (infinitive, gerund, participle) do not.
### The Infinitive
@tree Bare infinitive (no 'to') is used after
Bare infinitive
  Modals
  let, make, bid, have – Let him go
  Verbs of perception – see, watch, hear
  had better, would rather
  'Why' – Why wait here?
  than/but/except joining infinitives
@end

- to + V1; as noun ("To study is important") or purpose ("She came here to study").
- Bare infinitive (no 'to'): after modals; after let, make, bid, have ("Let him go", "She made him wait"); after verbs of perception (see, watch, hear – "I saw him cross the road"); after had better, would rather; after 'Why' ("Why wait here?"); after than/but/except joining infinitives.
- Avoid split infinitive: "I remember not to stay there" (not "to not stay").
- Passive infinitive (to be + V3): "Ramesh wants to be promoted by his boss."
### The Gerund
- V1 + ing used as a noun (verbal noun): "Smoking is injurious to health", "I enjoy watching movies."
### Gerund Golden Rules
@tree Use a gerund
Gerund (V-ing) after
  Prepositions – fond of reading
  Possessive adjectives – your waiting
  'to' as preposition
    look forward to, be used to
    accustomed to, with a view to
    addicted to, devoted to, object to, prone to
  Certain verbs/phrases
    enjoy, avoid, admit, deny, finish
    stop, suggest, consider
    can't help, it's no use, it is worth
@end

- After prepositions: "I am fond of reading books" (not "of to read").
- After possessive adjectives: "I appreciate your waiting for me."
- After phrases where 'to' is a preposition: look forward to, be used to, accustomed to, with a view to, addicted to, devoted to, object to, prone to – "I am looking forward to meeting you."
- After enjoy, avoid, admit, deny, finish, stop, suggest, consider, can't help, it's no use, it is worth – "He avoided going there."
### The Participle
- Present participle (V-ing, active): a rolling stone, a crying baby; Past participle (V3, passive): a broken chair, the stolen car.
- Dangling participle error: "Walking in the park, a dog bit Radhika" ✗ → "Walking in the park, Radhika was bitten by a dog" ✓.
- Perfect participle: "Having finished my homework, I went out."
### Gerund vs Infinitive – Meaning Change
|Verb|+ Gerund|+ Infinitive|
|Stop|quit the action (stopped smoking)|stop in order to (stopped to smoke)|
|Remember|recall a past action|not forget to do (remember to post the letter)|
|Forget|forget a past action|fail to do|
|Try|experiment (try reading this)|make an effort (tried to lift the box)|
|Regret|sorry for past action (regret telling her)|sorry to announce (regret to inform you)|

## Prepositions
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Prepositions
  In / On / At
    IN: large areas, longer periods
    ON: surfaces, days and dates
    AT: specific points, clock time
  Prepositions of Movement
    to, towards, into, onto
    from, off, out of
    across, along, through
  Confusing Pairs
    between two; among a group
    since = point; for = period
    despite never takes 'of'
    by = doer; with = instrument
  Fixed Prepositions
    afraid of; addicted to; good at
    angry with a person, at a thing
    abide by; refrain from
@end

### In / On / At
@pyramid IN → ON → AT (general to specific)
IN – large areas, longer periods (in India, in 2024)
ON – surfaces, days and dates (on Monday)
AT – specific points, clock time (at 5 PM)
@end

|Preposition|Place|Time|
|IN|large/enclosed areas – in India, in the room|longer periods – in January, in 2024, in summer|
|ON|surfaces/lines – on the table, on the road|days and dates – on Monday, on 15th August|
|AT|specific points – at the bus stop, at home|clock time, points – at 5 PM, at noon, at night|
- in the morning/evening/afternoon; at night/noon/dawn/dusk; "on a cold evening"; "on the morning of the wedding".
### Prepositions of Movement
@tree Prepositions of movement
Movement
  Where to
    to – destination
    towards – direction
    into – outside → inside
    onto – onto a surface
  Where from
    from – source
    off – fell off the ladder
    out of
  Path
    across – one side to other
    along – parallel
    through – tunnel/forest
@end

- to (destination), towards (direction), into (outside → inside), onto (onto a surface), from (source), off (fell off the ladder), out of, across (one side to other), along (parallel), through (tunnel/forest).
### Confusing Preposition Pairs
|Pair|Rule|Example|
|Between / Among|between two (named); among a group|between Ram and Shyam; among the children|
|Beside / Besides|beside = next to; besides = in addition to|Besides English, I teach Maths|
|Since / For|since = point of time; for = period|since 2010; for two years|
|Despite / In spite of|despite never takes 'of'|Despite the rain...|
|By / With|by = doer/transport; with = instrument|written by Ram with a pen; by bus|
|Agree with / to / on|with a person; to a proposal; on a matter|agree with you; agreed to my proposal|
|Die of / from / in|of a disease; from a cause; in an accident|died of cancer; from injuries; in a crash|
- No preposition before today, tomorrow, home: "I will go home tomorrow."
### Fixed Prepositions
@tree Fixed prepositions
Fixed prepositions
  of – accused, afraid, aware, proud, fond
  to – addicted, accustomed, prone, injurious
  at – good, bad, expert, brilliant
  with – angry with a person; comply with
  by – abide by
  from – refrain, abstain, prevent
  deal in (business) / deal with (person)
  anxious for (eager) / about (worried)
@end

- of: accused, afraid, aware, proud, fond; to: addicted, accustomed, prone, injurious ("injurious to health").
- at: good, bad, expert, brilliant ("good at Maths"); angry with a person, at a thing.
- abide by, comply with; refrain/abstain/prevent from; deal in (business), deal with (person/problem); anxious for (eager), about (worried).

## Tenses
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Tenses
  Present Tense
    Simple: habits, universal truths
    Continuous: now, these days
    Perfect: just, already, yet
    Perfect Continuous: since/for
  Past and Future Tense
    Simple Past: V2; did + V1
    Past Perfect = earlier of two past actions
    will be + V-ing; will have + V3
  Tense Rules and Traps
    Stative verbs: no continuous
    Since (point) vs For (period)
    If/when clause: present, no 'will'
    By the time + Past → Past Perfect
    It's high time + subject + V2
@end

### Present Tense
@tree Present tense forms
Present Tense
  Simple Present – S + V1/s/es
    habits, universal truths, proverbs
    always, daily, never
  Present Continuous – is/am/are + V-ing
    now, these days, personal future plans
  Present Perfect – has/have + V3
    just, already, yet, so far, recently
    never with yesterday, ago, last year
  Present Perfect Continuous – has/have been + V-ing
    needs since/for
@end

- Simple Present (S + V1/s/es): habits, universal truths ("The Earth revolves around the Sun"), proverbs, fixed official plans; hints: always, daily, never.
- Present Continuous (is/am/are + V-ing): now, these days; personal future plans; annoying habits with 'always'.
- Present Perfect (has/have + V3): just, already, yet, so far, recently, lately.
! Never use Present Perfect with yesterday, ago, last year – "He came yesterday" (not "has come").
- Present Perfect Continuous (has/have been + V-ing): needs since/for – "She has been studying for two hours."
### Past and Future Tense
@tree Past and future forms
Past and Future
  Past
    Simple Past – V2; did + V1
    Past Continuous – was/were + V-ing
    Past Perfect – had + V3
    Past Perfect Continuous – had been + V-ing
  Future
    Simple – will + V1
    Continuous – will be + V-ing
    Perfect – will have + V3
    Perfect Continuous – will have been + V-ing
@end

- Simple Past (V2; did + V1): "She did not attend" (not "attended").
- Past Continuous (was/were + V-ing): "I was writing a letter when she came."
- Past Perfect (had + V3) = earlier of two past actions: "The patient had died before the doctor came."
- Past Perfect Continuous: "had been studying for two weeks before..."
- Future: will + V1; will be + V-ing ("This time tomorrow I will be travelling"); will have + V3 ("by the time..."); will have been + V-ing.
### Tense Rules and Traps
- Stative verbs (know, believe, love, hate, own, have-possession, seem, taste, smell) not in continuous: "I know him", "The milk tastes sweet."
- Since (point – since Monday, since last week) vs For (period – for 3 hours, for the last two months).
- If/when/unless/as soon as clause: present tense, no 'will' – "If you work hard, you will succeed."
- By the time + Present → Future Perfect; By the time + Past → Past Perfect.
- It's (high) time + subject + V2: "It's high time you started studying."

## Mood and Conditional Sentences
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Mood and Conditionals
  Moods
    Indicative: facts/questions
    Imperative: commands, V1
    Subjunctive: wishes, unreal situations
    Present subjunctive: suggest that + V1
    Past subjunctive: I wish I were
  Conditional Types
    Zero: If + present, present
    First: If + present, will + V1
    Second: If + V2/were, would + V1
    Third: If + had V3, would have + V3
  Inversion and Rules
    Should you / Were I / Had he
    Unless/Until: no other negative
    Lest + should
@end

### Moods
@tree Moods
Mood
  Indicative – facts/questions
  Imperative – commands, V1
  Subjunctive – wishes, suggestions, unreal
    Present subjunctive → base form V1
      advise, demand, insist, suggest...
      It is necessary that he go there
    Past subjunctive → were/V2
      I wish / as if / as though
      I wish I were a king
@end

- Indicative (facts/questions), Imperative (commands, V1), Subjunctive (wishes, suggestions, unreal situations).
- Present subjunctive: after advise, demand, insist, recommend, suggest, propose, urge and "It is necessary/important/essential that" → base form V1: "It is necessary that he go there"; "The doctor advised that she rest."
- Past subjunctive: I wish / as if / as though + were/V2 – "I wish I were a king", "He talks as if he were the boss"; past regret: "I wish I had worked harder."
### Conditional Types
@tree Conditionals: real vs unreal
Conditional sentences
  Real
    Zero – facts: If you heat ice, it melts
    First – real future: If it rains, I will not go
  Unreal
    Second – imaginary present/future: If I were a bird
    Third – unreal past (regret): If you had studied
  Inversion (no 'if')
    Should you see him...
    Were I a king...
    Had he come...
@end

|Type|Use|Structure|Example|
|Zero|facts|If + present, present|If you heat ice, it melts.|
|First|real future possibility|If + present, will + V1|If it rains, I will not go.|
|Second|imaginary present/future|If + V2/were, would + V1|If I were a bird, I would fly.|
|Third|unreal past (regret)|If + had V3, would have + V3|If you had studied, you would have passed.|
- Inversion: Should you see him... / Were I a king... / Had he come...
- Unless/Until never take another negative; Lest + should.

## Verbs and Modals
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Verbs and Modals
  Primary Auxiliaries
    Be: continuous, passive, linking
    Do: negatives/questions
    Have: perfect tenses, possession
    Intransitive verbs cannot be passive
  Modals
    Golden rule: modal + V1
    Can/Could, May/Might
    Shall/Should, Will/Would
    Must, Ought to
    Used to + V1 vs be used to + V-ing
    Need/Dare without 'to'
  Confusing Verb Pairs
    Rise (intransitive) / Raise (transitive)
    Lie, lay, lain / Lay, laid, laid
    Fall / Fell (cut down)
    Hang: hung (object) / hanged (execute)
    Affect (verb) / Effect (noun)
@end

### Primary Auxiliaries and Verb Types
@tree Primary auxiliaries
Primary auxiliaries
  Be
    Helping: continuous (be + V-ing), passive (be + V3)
    Main: linking – She is a doctor
  Do
    Helping: negatives/questions in simple tenses
    Main: I did my homework
  Have
    Helping: perfect tenses
    Main: possession, Let's have lunch
@end

- Be: continuous (be + V-ing) and passive (be + V3); as linking main verb – "She is a doctor."
- Do: negatives/questions in simple tenses; as main verb – "I did my homework."
- Have: perfect tenses; as main verb – possession, "Let's have lunch."
- Transitive verbs take a direct object, no preposition: "He attacked the enemy" (not "attacked on"); intransitive verbs cannot be passive ("The baby was laughed" ✗).
### Modals
@tree Modals and their uses (modal + V1)
Modals
  Can / Could
    Can – ability/permission
    Could – past ability, polite request
  May / Might
    May – possibility, formal permission
    Might – remote possibility
  Shall / Should
    Shall – I/We future, suggestions
    Should – advice; after lest
  Will / Would
    Will – future, determination
    Would – polite request, past habit
  Must / Ought to
    Must – strong obligation/probability
    Ought to – moral duty
  Need / Dare
    without 'to' in negative/interrogative
@end

- Golden rule: modal + V1 – "He can go", "He should go".
- Can (ability/permission), Could (past ability, polite request, weak possibility); don't use "can be able to".
- May (possibility, formal permission – "May I come in?"), Might (past of may, remote possibility); no 'likely/possibly' with may/might.
- Shall (I/We future, suggestions), Should (advice; after lest).
- Will (future, determination), Would (past of will, polite request, past habit, conditional main clause).
- Must (strong obligation/probability), Ought to (moral duty – "We ought to respect our elders").
- Used to + V1 = past habit; be/get used to + V-ing = accustomed to ("I am used to working hard").
- Need/Dare as modals in negative/interrogative without 'to': "You need not worry", "How dare you!"
### Confusing Verb Pairs
|Verb|Forms|Meaning|
|Rise|rise, rose, risen|to get up (intransitive) – The sun rises|
|Raise|raise, raised, raised|to lift (transitive) – He raised his hand|
|Lie|lie, lay, lain|to recline (intransitive)|
|Lay|lay, laid, laid|to put/lay eggs (transitive)|
|Lie|lie, lied, lied|to tell a lie|
|Fall|fall, fell, fallen|to drop (intransitive)|
|Fell|fell, felled, felled|to cut down (transitive) – felled the tree|
|Hang|hang, hung, hung|to suspend an object|
|Hang|hang, hanged, hanged|to execute by hanging|
|Found|found, founded, founded|to establish (vs find, found, found)|
- Affect (verb) = to influence; Effect (noun) = result.

## Voice
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Voice
  Active to Passive Rules
    Swap subject and object
    Main verb → V3 + form of 'be'
    Tense stays the same
    Perfect Continuous: not converted
  Modals, Infinitives, Imperatives
    Modal + be + V3
    Infinitive: to + be + V3
    Imperative: Let ... be + V3
    You are advised/ordered to...
  Special Cases
    Intransitive verbs: no passive
    Two objects: two passives
    Who → By whom
    Quasi-passive: Honey tastes sweet
@end

### Active to Passive – Rules
@flow Active → Passive conversion
Swap subject and object
Main verb → V3
Add form of 'be' as per tense
Add 'by' (sometimes to, with, at, in)
Keep the same tense
Ram killed Ravan → Ravan was killed by Ram
@end

- Swap subject and object; main verb → V3; add a form of 'be' as per tense; use 'by' (sometimes to, with, at, in); tense stays the same.
|Tense|Passive structure|
|Simple Present|is/am/are + V3|
|Present Continuous|is/am/are + being + V3|
|Present Perfect|has/have + been + V3|
|Simple Past|was/were + V3|
|Past Continuous|was/were + being + V3|
|Past Perfect|had + been + V3|
|Simple Future|will/shall + be + V3|
|Future Perfect|will have + been + V3|
|Modals|modal + be + V3|
- Example: "Ram killed Ravan" → "Ravan was killed by Ram."
- Perfect Continuous tenses and Future Continuous are generally not converted to passive.
### Passive of Modals, Infinitives, Imperatives
@tree Passive patterns
Passive patterns
  Modal – modal + be + V3
    This box can be lifted by him
  Perfect modal – should have been + V3
  Infinitive – to + be + V3
    This work is to be done by me
  Imperative
    Let + object + be + V3 – Let the door be shut
    You are ordered/advised to...
  Let – Let the match be played by him
@end

- Modal: "He can lift this box" → "This box can be lifted by him"; perfect modal: "He should have been helped by you."
- Infinitive: to + be + V3 – "This work is to be done by me."
- Imperative: "Shut the door" → "Let the door be shut" / "You are ordered to shut the door"; "Work hard" → "You are advised to work hard."
- Let: "Let him play the match" → "Let the match be played by him."
### Voice – Special Cases
@flow Passive of a question
Who wrote this letter?
Who → By whom
By whom was this letter written?
@end

- Intransitive verbs (go, sleep, die) have no passive.
- Two objects: "He gave me a book" → "I was given a book by him" / "A book was given to me by him."
- Questions: "Who wrote this letter?" → "By whom was this letter written?"; "Did you finish the work?" → "Was the work finished by you?"
- Quasi-passive: "Honey tastes sweet" → "Honey is sweet when it is tasted."

## Narration
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Narration
  Basic Rules
    SON rule for pronouns
    now → then, today → that day
    Tense changes only if reporting verb is past
    Present → Past; Past → Past Perfect
    will → would; can → could
  By Sentence Type
    Assertive: told + that
    Interrogative: asked + if/whether
    Imperative: ordered/advised + to
    Exclamatory: exclaimed + that
    Optative: wished/prayed + that
    Let us → suggested that
  No Tense Change
    Universal truths, proverbs
    Historical facts
    Reporting verb present/future
@end

### Direct and Indirect Speech – Basic Rules
@flow Direct → Indirect conversion
Change reporting verb (said to → told/asked)
Change pronouns – SON rule
Check reporting verb: past? → change tense
Change time/place words (now → then)
@end

@tree SON rule for pronouns
Pronoun in reported speech
  1st person → as per Subject
  2nd person → as per Object
  3rd person → No change
@end

@flow Tense backshift (reporting verb past)
Simple Present → Simple Past
Simple Past / Present Perfect → Past Perfect
Past Perfect → No change
@end

- Pronoun (SON rule): 1st person → as per Subject; 2nd person → as per Object; 3rd person → No change.
- Time/place: now → then, today → that day, this → that, these → those, here → there, yesterday → the previous day, tomorrow → the next day.
- Tense changes only when the reporting verb is past:
|Direct|Indirect|
|Simple Present|Simple Past|
|Present Continuous|Past Continuous|
|Present Perfect|Past Perfect|
|Simple Past|Past Perfect|
|Past Continuous|Past Perfect Continuous|
|Past Perfect|No change|
|will/shall|would|
|can/may/must|could/might/had to|
### Narration by Sentence Type
@tree Reporting verb by sentence type
Sentence type
  Assertive → told + that
  Interrogative → asked
    yes/no → if/whether
    Wh- word acts as conjunction
  Imperative → ordered/requested/advised/forbade + to
  Exclamatory → exclaimed with joy/sorrow + that
  Optative → wished/prayed + that
  Let us → suggested/proposed that
  Good morning → wished; Good-bye → bade
@end

- Assertive: said to → told, conjunction 'that' – He said that he was busy.
- Interrogative: said to → asked; yes/no → if/whether ("She asked me if I was ready"); Wh- word acts as conjunction ("He asked her where she lived").
- Imperative: ordered/requested/advised/forbade + to/not to – "The doctor advised the patient to quit smoking"; "My father forbade me to waste my time."
- Exclamatory: exclaimed with joy/sorrow + that – "He exclaimed with sorrow that he was ruined."
- Optative: wished/prayed + that – "The saint prayed that God might bless me."
- Let us → suggested/proposed that they should...; Good morning → wished; Good-bye → bade.
! No tense change for universal truths, habitual facts, proverbs, historical facts, or when reporting verb is present/future (says, will say).

## Question Tags
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Question Tags
  Basic Rules
    Positive statement → negative tag
    Negative statement → positive tag
    Same tense; tag subject a pronoun
    Simple present: don't/doesn't
    Simple past: didn't
  Special Subjects
    This/That → it; These/Those → they
    Everyone/someone → they
    Something/nothing → it
    There is → isn't there?
  Special Cases
    Imperative → will you?
    Let's → shall we?
    hardly, seldom, never → positive tag
    I am → aren't I?
    Complex sentence: follows main clause
@end

### Question Tag Rules
@flow Making a question tag
Is the statement positive or negative?
Positive → negative tag; negative → positive tag
Repeat the helping verb (or don't/doesn't/didn't)
Same tense; subject as a pronoun
She is working, isn't she?
@end

@tree Tag subject
Statement subject → tag pronoun
  This / That → it
  These / Those → they
  Everyone / someone / no one → they
  Something / nothing → it
  There is → isn't there?
@end

- Positive statement → negative tag (She is working, isn't she?); negative statement → positive tag (He is not coming, is he?).
- Same tense; tag subject always a pronoun; negative tag contracted.
- Simple present: don't/doesn't; simple past: didn't; otherwise repeat the helping verb.
- Imperative: will you?/won't you?; "Don't make a noise, will you?"; Let's → shall we?
- This/That → it; These/Those → they; There is → isn't there?
- Everyone/someone/no one → they (plural verb): "Everyone has come, haven't they?"; something/nothing → it.
- Negative words (hardly, seldom, never, no, few, little) → positive tag: "He seldom comes here, does he?"
- I am → aren't I?; I am not → am I?
- need/dare as main verb → doesn't he?; as modal → need you?/dare he?
- Complex sentence: tag follows the main clause – "I think that he is right, don't I?"

## Superfluous Expressions
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Superfluous Expressions
  Redundant Words to Remove
    'back' after return, retreat, recall
    'again' after repeat, rebuild
    flee (not flee away)
    consensus (not consensus of opinion)
    No preposition after discuss, await, comprise
  Common Incorrect Usages
    Passing marks → Pass marks
    Cent percent → Hundred percent
    Yesterday night → Last night
    Mutual friend → Common friend
    The reason ... was that
    Forbade me to go
@end

### Redundant Words to Remove
@tree Redundant words
Redundant words
  'back' – return, retreat, reimburse, recall, recede
  'again' – repeat, reiterate, rebuild, rewrite
  Extra word with noun
    consensus of opinion, mutual agreement
    final conclusion, true fact, two twins
  Extra preposition
    discuss/describe about, await for
    comprise of, investigate into, despite of
    attack on, emphasize/stress on
@end

- 'back' after return, retreat, reimburse, recall, recede; 'again' after repeat, reiterate, rebuild, rewrite.
- flee (not 'flee away'); exceed (not 'exceed more than').
- consensus (not 'consensus of opinion'), agreement (not 'mutual agreement'), blunder (no big/small), conclusion (no 'final'), fact (no 'true'), twins (no 'two'), cousin (no brother/sister).
- No preposition after discuss, describe (about), await (for), comprise (of), investigate (into), despite (of), attack (on), emphasize/stress (on).
### Common Incorrect Usages
|Incorrect|Correct|
|Passing marks|Pass marks|
|Dressing sense|Dress sense|
|An English teacher|A teacher of English|
|My family members|The members of my family|
|To see a dream|To have a dream / To dream|
|Cent percent|Hundred percent|
|Yesterday night|Last night|
|Mutual friend|Common friend|
|The reason ... was because|The reason ... was that|
|Supposing if|Supposing / If (one only)|
|Forbade me not to go|Forbade me to go|
|Left with bag and baggage|Left bag and baggage|

## Noun
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Noun
  Types of Nouns
    Proper, Common, Collective
    Material, Abstract
  Plural Rules
    -es after s, ss, sh, ch, x, z
    consonant + y → ies
    f/fe → ves (but chiefs, roofs)
    sons-in-law, passers-by
  Noun Number Hotspots
    Uncountable: furniture, advice, information
    Always plural: scissors, trousers
    Look plural, singular: news, mathematics
    Same both: sheep, deer, series
  Possessive Case
    plural not ending in s + 's (children's)
    non-living → 'of' (except a week's leave)
    Ram and Shyam's shop
  Confusing Nouns
    Weather vs Climate
    Damage vs Damages
@end

### Types of Nouns
@tree Types of nouns
Noun
  Proper – Prashant, India, Ganga
  Common – boy, city
  Collective – jury, army, team
    unit → singular; individually → plural
  Material – gold, water (uncountable, no a/an)
  Abstract – honesty, poverty (uncountable)
@end

- Proper (Prashant, India, Ganga – capital letter, usually no article), Common (boy, city), Collective (jury, army, team – singular if acting as unit, plural if individually), Material (gold, water – uncountable, no a/an), Abstract (honesty, poverty – uncountable).
### Plural Rules
@tree Plural rules
Plural
  Regular
    -s – boys
    -es after s, ss, sh, ch, x, z – boxes
    consonant + y → ies – cities
    vowel + y → s – toys
    -o → es – heroes (but photos, pianos)
    f/fe → ves – wives (but chiefs, roofs)
  Irregular – men, teeth, feet, mice
  Foreign – criteria, phenomena, syllabi
  Compound – sons-in-law, passers-by
@end

- -s (boys); -es after s, ss, sh, ch, x, z (boxes); consonant + y → ies (cities); vowel + y → s (toys); -o → es (heroes, mangoes; but photos, pianos); f/fe → ves (wives, leaves; but chiefs, roofs).
- Irregular: men, teeth, feet, mice; foreign: criteria, phenomena, syllabi; compound: sons-in-law, passers-by.
### Noun Number Hotspots
@tree Noun number groups
Number hotspots
  Always uncountable (singular verb)
    furniture, advice, information, luggage
    scenery, machinery, stationery, evidence
  Always plural
    scissors, trousers, spectacles
    thanks, riches, savings, archives
  Look plural but singular
    news, mathematics, physics, politics
    measles, mumps, billiards
  Same in singular & plural
    sheep, deer, fish, series, species
    innings, headquarters, means
@end

- Always uncountable (singular verb, no plural): furniture, advice, information, luggage, baggage, scenery, poetry, machinery, stationery, jewellery, bread, work, evidence – "The scenery of Kashmir is beautiful."
- Always plural: scissors, trousers, jeans, spectacles, binoculars, thanks, regards, riches, assets, savings, archives – "a pair of scissors".
- Look plural but singular: news, mathematics, physics, politics, economics, measles, mumps, billiards – "Mathematics is a difficult subject."
- Same in singular & plural: sheep, deer, fish, series, species, innings, headquarters, means; 'fishes' = different species.
### Possessive Case and Confusing Nouns
- Singular + 's; plural ending in s + '; plural not ending in s + 's (children's); non-living → 'of' (except time, weight, distance – a week's leave).
- Compound noun: 's on last word; common possession: 's on last noun (Ram and Shyam's shop); separate possession: 's on each.
- Habit (individual) vs Custom (society); Weather (short-term) vs Climate (long-term); House (building) vs Home (belonging); Room (space to occupy); Damage (harm) vs Damages (compensation).

## Articles
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Articles
  A / An / The
    A/An: singular countable, indefinite
    The: definite, specific
    Choice by sound: a university, an hour
  Uses of 'The'
    Second mention, unique things
    Rivers, oceans, mountain ranges, deserts
    Superlatives, ordinals, holy books
    the poor; the better of the two
  No 'The'
    Lakes, continents, single peaks
    Countries except the USA, the UK
  Omission of Articles
    Proper, material, abstract nouns
    Languages, sports, meals in general
    School for primary purpose
    by bus, by air
@end

### A / An / The
@tree A or An – decided by sound
Article choice by sound
  A – consonant sound
    a university, a one-rupee note, a European
  An – vowel sound
    an hour, an honest man, an heir, an MLA
@end

- A/An: indefinite, only with singular countable nouns; The: definite, specific noun.
- Choice by sound: a university, a one-rupee note, a European; an hour, an honest man, an heir, an MLA.
- Zero article: "Honesty is the best policy."
### Uses of 'The'
@tree The or no 'the' with names
Geographical names
  Use 'the'
    rivers – the Ganga
    oceans/seas
    mountain ranges – the Himalayas
    groups of islands
    deserts – the Thar
    canals – the Suez Canal
  No 'the'
    lakes, continents, streets
    single peaks – Mount Everest
    single islands
    countries (except the USA, the UK)
@end

- Specific reference, second mention ("I saw a lion. The lion was sleeping"), unique things (the sun, the earth, the sky).
- Use 'the': rivers (the Ganga), oceans/seas, mountain ranges (the Himalayas), groups of islands, deserts (the Thar), canals (the Suez Canal).
- No 'the': lakes, continents, single peaks (Mount Everest), single islands, streets; countries except the USA, the UK, the Netherlands, the Philippines.
- Superlatives (the tallest), ordinals (the first chapter), holy books (the Gita), newspapers (the Hindu), monuments (the Taj Mahal), ships/trains/hotels, parties/departments.
- Adjective as class: "We should help the poor"; comparative of choice: "the better of the two"; "The more you earn, the more you spend."
### Omission of Articles
@tree General vs specific
Article omitted or used
  General sense → no article
    Gold is a precious metal
    He goes to school (primary purpose)
  Specific → 'the'
    The gold from this mine...
    went to the school to meet the principal
    The dinner we had last night
@end

- Proper nouns, material/abstract nouns in general sense ("Gold is a precious metal" vs "The gold from this mine..."), languages and subjects, sports, meals in general ("The dinner we had last night" is specific).
- Institutions for primary purpose: "He goes to school"; secondary purpose: "went to the school to meet the principal".
- Transport with 'by': by bus, by air.

## Subject-Verb Agreement
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Subject-Verb Agreement
  Core SVA Rules
    Singular subject → singular verb
    Ignore along with, as well as
    'And' → plural (except single idea)
    Or/nor → nearer subject
    Each, every, everyone → singular
    Collective: unit singular, individuals plural
    There is/are → following subject
  SVA Hotspots
    The number of → singular
    A number of → plural
    Fractions: noun after 'of'
    Distance/money/time unit → singular
    One of my friends is
@end

### Core SVA Rules
@flow Finding the right verb
Find the real subject
Ignore nouns in prepositional phrases (along with, as well as)
Joined by 'and'? → plural (unless single idea)
Joined by or/nor? → agree with nearer subject
Each/every/everyone/neither? → singular
@end

- Singular subject → singular verb (is, was, has, plays); plural → plural verb.
- Ignore nouns in prepositional phrases (of, with, along with, as well as, besides): "The quality of the apples is not good"; "The captain, along with his players, has arrived."
- 'And' → plural, except single idea/person: "Bread and butter is my favourite breakfast"; "The actor and director is here" vs "The actor and the director are here."
- Or/nor/either...or/neither...nor → verb agrees with the nearer subject: "Neither the students nor the teacher was present."
- Each, every, everyone, someone, anyone, no one, either, neither → singular: "Each of the boys was given a prize."
- Collective noun: unit → singular ("The jury has reached its verdict"); individuals → plural ("The jury were divided").
- There is/There are → verb agrees with the following subject.
### SVA Hotspots
@tree Singular or plural verb
Hotspots
  Singular verb
    The number of students is
    Ten miles is a long distance
    Five thousand rupees is a large sum
    One of my friends is going
    Slow and steady wins the race
  Plural verb
    A number of students are
    My spectacles are broken
  Depends on noun after 'of'
    Two-thirds of the work has
    Fifty percent of the students have
@end

- "The number of students is increasing" (singular) vs "A number of students are absent" (plural).
- Fractions/percentages: verb agrees with the noun after 'of' – "Two-thirds of the work has been completed"; "Fifty percent of the students have passed."
- Distance/money/time as a unit → singular: "Ten miles is a long distance"; "Five thousand rupees is a large sum."
- "One of my friends is going"; "Slow and steady wins the race"; "My spectacles are broken."

## Determiners
### 🧠 एक नज़र में
@tree
Determiners
  Some/Any, Much/Many
    Some: affirmative, offers
    Any: negatives, questions
    Much + uncountable; Many + plural
    Many a + singular noun + singular verb
  Few / Little
    little, a little: uncountable
    few, a few: plural countable
    little/few = almost none
    a little/a few = some
  Each/Every, Another/Other, All/Both
    Each: individually, can be pronoun
    Every: collectively, not a pronoun
    Another + singular; other + plural
    Both + plural verb
    enough money; rich enough
@end

### Some/Any, Much/Many
@tree Some/Any, Much/Many
Determiners of quantity
  Some – affirmative, offers/requests
  Any – negatives, questions
    after hardly, scarcely, barely, seldom, without
  Much – uncountable (much time)
  Many – plural countable (many cars)
    Many a + singular noun + singular verb
    A great many + plural noun + plural verb
@end

- Some: affirmative sentences and offers/requests ("Would you like some tea?"); Any: negatives and questions.
- hardly, scarcely, barely, seldom, without → any: "He has hardly any money."
- Much + uncountable ("much time"); Many + plural countable ("many cars").
- Many a + singular noun + singular verb ("Many a student has failed"); A great many + plural noun + plural verb.
### Few / Little
@tree Few/Little by noun type
Few / Little
  Uncountable nouns
    little – almost none (negative)
    a little – some (positive)
    the little – all that little available
  Plural countable nouns
    few – almost none (negative)
    a few – some (positive)
    the few – all those few available
@end

|Determiner|Noun|Meaning|
|little|uncountable|almost none (negative) – little hope|
|a little|uncountable|some (positive)|
|the little|uncountable|all that little is available|
|few|plural countable|almost none (negative)|
|a few|plural countable|some (positive)|
|the few|plural countable|all those few available|
### Each/Every, Another/Other, All/Both
- Each (two or more, individually; can be pronoun – "Each of the boys"); Every (more than two, collectively; not a pronoun).
- Another + singular countable; other + plural/uncountable; the other = second of two or the remaining ones; others = pronoun.
- All/Half: verb agrees with the noun; Both (two) + plural verb; the whole + singular countable ("the whole cake").
- Enough before a noun ("enough money") but after an adjective ("rich enough").
